   - Path 5: B -> N -> Y -> M -> F -> K -> G -> H
   - Path 6: B -> N -> C -> I -> P -> J -> D -> E -> F -> K -> G -> H

2. **Determine the minimum set of edges to remove:**
   - Removing edges (B, N), (E, L), and (G, H) will disconnect all paths between B and H.

To determine the minimum number of nodes that must be removed to disconnect the two black nodes (E and I), we need to analyze the paths between them and identify the critical nodes whose removal would eliminate all possible paths.

1. **Path Analysis:**
   - From E to I, we can identify the following paths:
     - E → H → B → C → O → I
     - E → H → B → C → N → O → I
     - E → H → B → C → N → Y → O → I
     - E → F → G → X → K → H → B → C → O → I
     - E → F → G → X → K → H → B → C → N → O → I
     - E → F → G → X → K → H → B → C → N → Y → O → I
     - E → M → L → J → P → I
     - E → M → L → J → D → P → I

2. **Critical Node Identification:**
   - By removing node E, we eliminate all paths starting from E.
   - By removing node I, we eliminate all paths ending at I.
   - However, E and I cannot be removed as per the problem constraints.
   - We need to find the minimum set of nodes that, when removed, will disconnect all paths between E and I.
   - Observing the paths, we see that node H is a critical node in multiple paths.
   - Additionally, node C is another critical node in multiple paths.
   - Finally, node P is critical in the paths involving M, L, J, and D.

3. **Minimum Cut Set:**
   - Removing nodes H and C will disconnect most of the paths.
   - However, paths through M, L, J, and P remain.
   - By removing node P, we eliminate the remaining paths.

Thus, the minimum number of nodes that need to be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes is 3.

Nodes to remove: 3, Cut Set: [H, C, P]

### Paths between E and J:
1. E - H - B - C - O - P - J
2. E - H - B - C - N - O - P - J
3. E - H - B - C - O - Y - P - J
4. E - H - I - P - J
5. E - F - G - X - K - L - M - J

### Cut Set Identification:
To disconnect E and J, we need to remove nodes such that no path between E and J remains. The optimal cut set should minimize the number of nodes removed.

### Removing a Single Node:
No single node can be removed to disconnect all paths between E and J because all paths involve different nodes.

### Removing Two Nodes:
We need to find at least two nodes whose removal will disconnect all paths between E and J. Consider the following pairs:

- Removing nodes {H, I}:
  - Paths 1, 2, and 3 are disconnected because H is removed.
  - Path 4 is disconnected because I is removed.
  - Path 5 remains, so this is not a valid cut set.
  
- Removing nodes {H, L}:
  - Paths 1, 2, and 3 are disconnected because H is removed.
  - Path 4 remains, so this is not a valid cut set.

- Removing nodes {H, P}:
  - Paths 1, 2, 3, and 4 are disconnected because either H or P is removed.
  - Path 5 remains, so this is not a valid cut set.

- Removing nodes {I, L}:
  - Path 4 is disconnected because I is removed.
  - Path 5 remains, so this is not a valid cut set.

### Removing Three Nodes:
We need to find at least three nodes whose removal will disconnect all paths between E and J. Consider the following triples:

- Removing nodes {H, I, L}:
  - Paths 1, 2, and 3 are disconnected because H is removed.
  - Path 4 is disconnected because I is removed.
  - Path 5 is disconnected because L is removed.

This set of nodes {H, I, L} satisfies the condition of completely disconnecting E and J.

### Conclusion:
The minimum number of nodes that must be removed is 3.

Nodes to remove: 3, Cut Set: [H, I, L]
